Mathlib.CategoryTheory.Limits.Cones

CategoryTheory.Functor.coconesdefMathlib.CategoryTheory.Limits.Cones, function or constant
Wording
the cocone of F (from the name)
English
Let $F$ be a functor of $J$ and $C$. Then the cocone of $F$ is a functor of $C$ and the set of sets defined as the composition of the constant of $J$ and the action of the Coyoneda and $\operatorname{op}$ applied to $F$.
Formula
$F \in \operatorname{Functor} (J , C)\vdash \operatorname{cocones} (F):= \operatorname{comp} (\operatorname{const} (J), \operatorname{obj} (\operatorname{coyoneda} , \operatorname{op} (F)))\in \operatorname{Functor} (C , \mathrm{Set})$
CategoryTheory.Functor.conesdefMathlib.CategoryTheory.Limits.Cones, function or constant
Wording
the cone of F (from the name)
English
Let $F$ be a functor of $J$ and $C$. Then the cone of $F$ is a functor of the set of opposites of $C$ and the set of sets defined as the composition of the opposite of the constant of $J$ and the action of the Yoneda and $F$.
Formula
$F \in \operatorname{Functor} (J , C)\vdash \operatorname{cones} (F):= \operatorname{comp} (\operatorname{op} (\operatorname{const} (J)), \operatorname{obj} (\operatorname{yoneda} , F))\in \operatorname{Functor} (\mathrm{Opposite} (C), \mathrm{Set})$
CategoryTheory.Functor.functorialityCompPostcomposedefMathlib.CategoryTheory.Limits.Cones, function or constant
Wording
the functoriality composition postcomposition of x3 (from the name)
English
Let $x_{3}$ be an isomorphism of $H$ and $\mathrm{H'}$. Then the functoriality composition postcomposition of $x_{3}$ is an isomorphism of the composition of the functoriality of $F$ and $H$ and the postcomposition of the left whisker of $F$ and the homomorphism of $x_{3}$ and the functoriality of $F$ and $\mathrm{H'}$.
Formula
$x_{3} \in \operatorname{Iso} (H , \mathrm{H'})\vdash \operatorname{functorialityCompPostcompose} (x_{3})\in \operatorname{Iso} (\operatorname{comp} (\operatorname{functoriality} (F , H), \operatorname{postcompose} (\operatorname{whiskerLeft} (F , \operatorname{hom} (x_{3})))), \operatorname{functoriality} (F , \mathrm{H'}))$
CategoryTheory.Functor.functorialityCompPrecomposedefMathlib.CategoryTheory.Limits.Cones, function or constant
Wording
the functoriality composition precomposition of x3 (from the name)
English
Let $x_{3}$ be an isomorphism of $H$ and $\mathrm{H'}$. Then the functoriality composition precomposition of $x_{3}$ is an isomorphism of the composition of the functoriality of $F$ and $H$ and the precomposition of the left whisker of $F$ and the inverse of $x_{3}$ and the functoriality of $F$ and $\mathrm{H'}$.
Formula
$x_{3} \in \operatorname{Iso} (H , \mathrm{H'})\vdash \operatorname{functorialityCompPrecompose} (x_{3})\in \operatorname{Iso} (\operatorname{comp} (\operatorname{functoriality} (F , H), \operatorname{precompose} (\operatorname{whiskerLeft} (F , \operatorname{inv} (x_{3})))), \operatorname{functoriality} (F , \mathrm{H'}))$
CategoryTheory.Functor.mapCoconedefMathlib.CategoryTheory.Limits.Cones, function or constant
Wording
the map cocone of H and c (from the name)
English
Let $H$ be a functor of $C$ and $D$. Let $c$ be a cocone of $F$. Then the map cocone of $H$ and $c$ is a cocone of the composition of $F$ and $H$ defined as the action of the functoriality of $F$ and $H$ and $c$.
Formula
$H \in \operatorname{Functor} (C , D), c \in \operatorname{Cocone} (F)\vdash \operatorname{mapCocone} (H , c):= \operatorname{obj} (\operatorname{functoriality} (F , H), c)\in \operatorname{Cocone} (\operatorname{comp} (F , H))$
CategoryTheory.Functor.mapCoconeInvdefMathlib.CategoryTheory.Limits.Cones, function or constant
Wording
the map cocone inverse of H and c (from the name)
English
Let $H$ be a functor of $C$ and $D$. Let $c$ be a cocone of the composition of $F$ and $H$. Then the map cocone inverse of $H$ and $c$ is a cocone of $F$ defined as the action of the inverse of the functoriality equivalence of $F$ and $\operatorname{asEquivalence}$ applied to $H$ and $c$.
Formula
$H \in \operatorname{Functor} (C , D), c \in \operatorname{Cocone} (\operatorname{comp} (F , H))\vdash \operatorname{mapCoconeInv} (H , c):= \operatorname{obj} (\operatorname{inverse} (\operatorname{functorialityEquivalence} (F , \operatorname{asEquivalence} (H))), c)\in \operatorname{Cocone} (F)$
CategoryTheory.Functor.mapCoconeInvMapCoconedefMathlib.CategoryTheory.Limits.Cones, function or constant
Wording
the map cocone inverse map cocone of H and c (from the name)
English
Let $H$ be a functor of $D$ and $C$. Let $c$ be a cocone of $F$. Then the map cocone inverse map cocone of $H$ and $c$ is an isomorphism of the map cocone inverse of $H$ and the map cocone of $H$ and $c$ and $c$ defined as the application of the inverse of the unit isomorphism of the functoriality equivalence of $F$ and $\operatorname{asEquivalence}$ applied to $H$ and $c$.
Formula
$H \in \operatorname{Functor} (D , C), c \in \operatorname{Cocone} (F)\vdash \operatorname{mapCoconeInvMapCocone} (H , c):= \operatorname{Iso} (\operatorname{symm} (\operatorname{unitIso} (\operatorname{functorialityEquivalence} (F , \operatorname{asEquivalence} (H)))), c)\in \operatorname{Iso} (\operatorname{mapCoconeInv} (H , \operatorname{mapCocone} (H , c)), c)$
CategoryTheory.Functor.mapCoconeMapCoconedefMathlib.CategoryTheory.Limits.Cones, function or constant
Wording
the map cocone map cocone of c (from the name)
English
Let $c$ be a cocone of $F$. Then the map cocone map cocone of $c$ is an isomorphism of the map cocone of $\mathrm{H'}$ and the map cocone of $H$ and $c$ and the map cocone of the composition of $H$ and $\mathrm{H'}$ and $c$.
Formula
$c \in \operatorname{Cocone} (F)\vdash \operatorname{mapCoconeMapCocone} (c)\in \operatorname{Iso} (\operatorname{mapCocone} (\mathrm{H'} , \operatorname{mapCocone} (H , c)), \operatorname{mapCocone} (\operatorname{comp} (H , \mathrm{H'}), c))$
CategoryTheory.Functor.mapCoconeMapCoconeInvdefMathlib.CategoryTheory.Limits.Cones, function or constant
Wording
the map cocone map cocone inverse of H and c (from the name)
English
Let $H$ be a functor of $D$ and $C$. Let $c$ be a cocone of the composition of $F$ and $H$. Then the map cocone map cocone inverse of $H$ and $c$ is an isomorphism of the map cocone of $H$ and the map cocone inverse of $H$ and $c$ and $c$ defined as the application of the counit isomorphism of the functoriality equivalence of $F$ and $\operatorname{asEquivalence}$ applied to $H$ and $c$.
Formula
$H \in \operatorname{Functor} (D , C), c \in \operatorname{Cocone} (\operatorname{comp} (F , H))\vdash \operatorname{mapCoconeMapCoconeInv} (H , c):= \operatorname{Iso} (\operatorname{counitIso} (\operatorname{functorialityEquivalence} (F , \operatorname{asEquivalence} (H))), c)\in \operatorname{Iso} (\operatorname{mapCocone} (H , \operatorname{mapCoconeInv} (H , c)), c)$
CategoryTheory.Functor.mapCoconeMorphismdefMathlib.CategoryTheory.Limits.Cones, function or constant
Wording
the map cocone morphism of H and f (from the name)
English
Let $H$ be a functor of $C$ and $D$. Let $f$ be a homomorphism of $\mathrm{c'}$ and $c$. Then the map cocone morphism of $H$ and $f$ is a homomorphism of the map cocone of $H$ and $\mathrm{c'}$ and the map cocone of $H$ and $c$ defined as the map of the functoriality of $F$ and $H$ and $f$.
Formula
$H \in \operatorname{Functor} (C , D), f \in \operatorname{Hom} (\mathrm{c'} , c)\vdash \operatorname{mapCoconeMorphism} (H , f):= \operatorname{map} (\operatorname{functoriality} (F , H), f)\in \operatorname{Hom} (\operatorname{mapCocone} (H , \mathrm{c'}), \operatorname{mapCocone} (H , c))$
CategoryTheory.Functor.mapCoconeOpdefMathlib.CategoryTheory.Limits.Cones, function or constant
Wording
the opposite cone of the image of G and t (from the docstring)
English
Let $G$ be a functor of $C$ and $D$. Let $t$ be a cocone of $F$. Then the opposite cone of the image of $G$ and $t$ is an isomorphism of the opposite of the map cocone of $G$ and $t$ and the map cone of the opposite of $G$ and the opposite of $t$.
Formula
$G \in \operatorname{Functor} (C , D), t \in \operatorname{Cocone} (F)\vdash \operatorname{mapCoconeOp} (G , t)\in \operatorname{Iso} (\operatorname{op} (\operatorname{mapCocone} (G , t)), \operatorname{mapCone} (\operatorname{op} (G), \operatorname{op} (t)))$

Wording

English

Wording
the equivalence of F (from the name)
English

Formula
$F \in \operatorname{Functor} (J , C)\vdash \operatorname{equiv} (F)\in \operatorname{Iso} (\operatorname{Cocone} (F), \Sigma (\lambda X . \operatorname{obj} (\operatorname{cocones} (F), X)))$

Wording

English

Formula
$e \in \operatorname{Equivalence} (K , J), x_{3} \in \operatorname{Iso} (\operatorname{comp} (\operatorname{functor} (e), F), G)\vdash \operatorname{equivalenceOfReindexing} (e , x_{3}):= \operatorname{trans} (\operatorname{whiskeringEquivalence} (e), \operatorname{precomposeEquivalence} (x_{3}))\in \operatorname{Equivalence} (\operatorname{Cocone} (F), \operatorname{Cocone} (G))$

CategoryTheory.Limits.Cocone.extdefMathlib.CategoryTheory.Limits.Cones, function or constant
Wording
the extension of x2 and w (from the name)
English
Let $x_{2}$ be an isomorphism of $\operatorname{pt}$ applied to $c$ and $\operatorname{pt}$ applied to $\mathrm{c'}$ such that $\mathrm{autoParam}$ holds for the proposition that for all elements $j$ of $J$, the composition of the component of the inclusion of $c$ and $j$ and the homomorphism of $x_{2}$ is equal to the component of the inclusion of $\mathrm{c'}$ and $j$ and $\mathrm{CategoryTheory\_\_Limits\_\_Cocone\_\_ext\_\_\_auto\_1}$. Formula

Let $c$ be a cocone of $F$. Let $f$ be a homomorphism of $\operatorname{pt}$ applied to $c$ and $X$. Then the extension of $c$ and $f$ is a cocone of $F$ defined as $\operatorname{mk}$ applied to $X$ and the composition of the inclusion of $c$ and the map of the constant of $J$ and $f$.
Formula
$c \in \operatorname{Cocone} (F), f \in \operatorname{Hom} (\operatorname{pt} (c), X)\vdash \operatorname{extend} (c , f):= \operatorname{mk} (X , \operatorname{comp} (\mathrm{CategoryTheory\_\_Limits\_\_Cocone\_\_\_u3b9\_} (c), \operatorname{map} (\operatorname{const} (J), f)))\in \operatorname{Cocone} (F)$
